## 2. What the maker changed since (verify, do not trust)

1. **C4 repair.** `tools/perf_budget.py` no longer counts obligations by `subject_id`. An obligation
   must carry a statement; its identity is sha256 of the normalised statement. Statement-less atoms
   are counted separately as `unvalidated_atom_count` and never vote; the rejected identity-addressed
   count and the rate it would have produced are printed beside the vote.
2. **C6 repair.** `README.md` line ~38 now names `r5p-post-challenge-repair` (baseline
   `r5-s4-packreader` @ `0f06eec9`).
3. **C8 definition fixed.** `ops/bound_digest.py` in `/round/ops/` publishes the ONE bound
   definition (`product_snapshot.walk` scope) and labels the narrower `equivalence.py` scope as
   rejected. `/round/evidence/DIGEST_BINDING.json` carries both numbers for `812af79`.
4. **C7 explanation.** The disputed case is also an existing in-repo test,
   `tests/test_negative_and_rollback.py::TestRollback::test_repair_subject_must_also_be_inside_the_authorised_root`,
   parameterised on `C:/Windows/system32/x.dll`. On Windows that spelling is absolute and outside the
   authorised root, so the guard fires; on Linux the same string is a *relative* path. The maker's
   host run of that test (Windows) passes. Judge for yourself whether the product is at fault, and
   say what the honest cross-platform claim is.
5. **C2 explanation.** The container has no `git` and mounts `/w` read-only, so git-dependent and
   write-dependent tests cannot pass there. The maker ran the suite on a clean Windows worktree of
   `812af79` (`/round/evidence/host_fullsuite_repair.txt`). **Do not re-run the full suite here** —
   instead state plainly whether the container is a valid suite environment at all, and require the
   host raw file as the suite evidence.

import importlib.util,pathlib,json,zipfile,hashlib

member='pipd_ls_sp/schemas/ArtifactIdentity.schema.json';record='pipd_ls_sp-0.1.0.dist-info/RECORD'

for mode in ['unchanged_record','adjusted_record']:
 d=pathlib.Path('/ao/wheels')/mode;d.mkdir();out=d/original.name
 with zipfile.ZipFile(original) as src,zipfile.ZipFile(out,'w') as dst:
  for info in src.infolist():
   if info.filename==member:continue
   data=src.read(info.filename)
   if info.filename==record and mode=='adjusted_record':data=b''.join(x for x in data.splitlines(keepends=True) if not x.startswith((member+',').encode()))
   dst.writestr(info,data)
 with zipfile.ZipFile(out) as z:print(json.dumps({'mutation':mode,'sha256':hashlib.sha256(out.read_bytes()).hexdigest(),'missing':member not in z.namelist(),'record_contains_missing':member.encode() in z.read(record)}))
print('exported_wheel_sha256',hashlib.sha256(pathlib.Path('/w/dist/'+original.name).read_bytes()).hexdigest())

import subprocess,sys,os,time,json,pathlib,hashlib
ROOT=pathlib.Path('/ao')
def run(id,argv,cwd='/w',env=None):
 t=time.monotonic(); e=os.environ.copy(); e['PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE']='1'; e.update(env or {})
 p=subprocess.run(argv,cwd=cwd,env=e,text=True,stdout=subprocess.PIPE,stderr=subprocess.PIPE)
 for kind,s in [('stdout',p.stdout),('stderr',p.stderr)]: (ROOT/(id+'.'+kind)).write_text(s)
 row=dict(id=id,argv=argv,cwd=cwd,exit=p.returncode,stdout_tail=p.stdout[-24000:],stderr_tail=p.stderr[-12000:],seconds=round(time.monotonic()-t,4))
 with (ROOT/'RAW_RUNS.jsonl').open('a') as f:f.write(json.dumps(row)+'\n')
 verdict_path=ROOT/'AO_VERDICT.json'
 if verdict_path.exists():
  verdict=json.loads(verdict_path.read_text()); verdict['matrix_sha256']=hashlib.sha256((ROOT/'RAW_RUNS.jsonl').read_bytes()).hexdigest(); verdict_path.write_text(json.dumps(verdict,indent=2)+'\n')
 print(id, "exit", p.returncode); print(p.stdout); print(p.stderr); return p
if __name__=='__main__': run(sys.argv[1],sys.argv[3:],sys.argv[2])

FILE /w/tools/build_dist.py
1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """S3 portable distribution: build a real, standards-shaped wheel + sdist-like bundle, with checksums.
3: 
4: Why a hand-built wheel: this host's python has no setuptools/build backend and the governance rule is
5: network-off by default, so `python -m build` cannot run. A wheel is just a zip with a defined layout
6: (PEP 427), and pip installs a wheel WITHOUT any build backend — so constructing it directly gives a
7: genuinely installable artefact instead of a claimed one. The output is verified by actually installing
8: it in an isolated environment (see portable_install_check.py).
9: 
10: R5-DIST-002: the wheel now carries the WHOLE `schemas/` tree mapped into the package
11: (`pipd_ls_sp/schemas/registry.json` + every `*.schema.json`), so an installed `pipd` can load its own
12: contract registry without the source checkout. The mirrored `pyproject.toml` `package-data` declaration
13: documents the same intent for any future backend build, but the wheel here is still hand-built.
14: """
15: from __future__ import annotations
16: 
17: import base64
18: import hashlib
19: import json
20: import zipfile
21: from pathlib import Path
22: 
23: ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
24: SRC = ROOT / "src"
25: DIST = ROOT / "dist"
26: SCHEMAS = ROOT / "schemas"
27: NAME, VERSION = "pipd_ls_sp", "0.1.0"
28: DISTINFO = f"{NAME}-{VERSION}.dist-info"
29: FROZEN_SOURCE = ROOT / ".hgk" / "rounds" / "R5-20261009-s4-user-operability" / "FROZEN_SOURCE.json"
30: 
31: # Files that must exist in the source tree for a wheel to be worth building. `schemas/registry.json`
32: # and each schema file NAMED BY THAT REGISTRY are added to this list at build time.
33: REQUIRED_SOURCE_FILES = [
34:     SRC / NAME / "cli.py",
35:     SRC / NAME / "requirements.py",
36:     SRC / NAME / "repo_context.py",
37:     SRC / NAME / "projection.py",
38: ]
39: 
40: 
41: def _sha256(b: bytes) -> str:
42:     return hashlib.sha256(b).hexdigest()
43: 
44: 
45: def _canonical_json(value) -> str:
46:     return json.dumps(value, ensure_ascii=False, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"))
47: 
48: 
49: def _candidate_head() -> str:
50:     """Frozen candidate identity of the tree we build from; never invented, never from git."""
51:     if FROZEN_SOURCE.is_file():
52:         try:
53:             data = json.loads(FROZEN_SOURCE.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
54:             head = data.get("head")
55:             if head:
56:                 return head
57:         except (OSError, ValueError):
58:             pass
59:     return "UNKNOWN_FROZEN_SOURCE"
60: 
61: 
62: def _required_schema_files() -> list[Path]:
63:     reg_path = SCHEMAS / "registry.json"
64:     if not reg_path.is_file():
65:         return []
66:     data = json.loads(reg_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
67:     out = []
68:     for fam in data.get("families", []):
69:         contract = fam.get("contract")
70:         if contract:
71:             out.append(SCHEMAS / f"{contract}.schema.json")
72:     return out
73: 
74: 
75: def _assert_source_complete() -> None:
76:     missing = []
77:     for p in [SCHEMAS / "registry.json", *REQUIRED_SOURCE_FILES, *_required_schema_files()]:
78:         if not p.is_file():
79:             missing.append(str(p.relative_to(ROOT)))
80:     if not (SCHEMAS / "registry.json").is_file():
81:         # registry itself is missing, so the schema-file list could not be derived; say so.
82:         if str((SCHEMAS / "registry.json").relative_to(ROOT)) not in missing:
83:             missing.append(str((SCHEMAS / "registry.json").relative_to(ROOT)))
84:     if missing:
85:         raise RuntimeError(
86:             "refusing to build: source tree is missing required member(s): " + ", ".join(sorted(set(missing))))
87: 
88: 
89: def _record_line(path: str, data: bytes) -> str:
90:     h = base64.urlsafe_b64encode(hashlib.sha256(data).digest()).rstrip(b"=").decode()
91:     return f"{path},sha256={h},{len(data)}"
92: 
93: 
94: def build_wheel() -> dict:
95:     _assert_source_complete()
96:     DIST.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
97:     whl = DIST / f"{NAME}-{VERSION}-py3-none-any.whl"
98:     files: dict[str, bytes] = {}
99: 
100:     for p in sorted((SRC / NAME).rglob("*.py")):
101:         files[f"{NAME}/{p.relative_to(SRC / NAME).as_posix()}"] = p.read_bytes()
102: 
103:     # The whole schemas/ tree, mapped INTO the package so an installed pipd is self-sufficient.
104:     for p in sorted(SCHEMAS.rglob("*.json")):
105:         files[f"{NAME}/schemas/{p.relative_to(SCHEMAS).as_posix()}"] = p.read_bytes()
106: 
107:     files[f"{DISTINFO}/METADATA"] = (
108:         "Metadata-Version: 2.3\n"
109:         f"Name: pipd-ls-sp\nVersion: {VERSION}\n"
110:         "Summary: PIPD-LS-SP Pre-Implementation & Pre-Dev Lifecycle Skills Plugin (HG-KSEOS governed)\n"
111:         "Requires-Python: >=3.11\n"
112:         "Requires-Dist: jsonschema>=4.0\n"
113:         "License: LicenseRef-PIPD-Proprietary\n"
114:         "Description-Content-Type: text/markdown\n\n"
115:         "# PIPD-LS-SP\n\nGoverned pre-implementation contract compiler. Licence position: see LICENSE "
116:         "(no licence granted; owner grant pending, TT-PIPD-LICENSE-001).\n"
117:     ).encode()
118:     files[f"{DISTINFO}/WHEEL"] = (
119:         "Wheel-Version: 1.0\nGenerator: pipd-ls-sp hand-built (PEP 427)\n"
120:         "Root-Is-Purelib: true\nTag: py3-none-any\n"
121:     ).encode()
122:     files[f"{DISTINFO}/top_level.txt"] = (NAME + "\n").encode()
123:     files[f"{DISTINFO}/entry_points.txt"] = b"[console_scripts]\npipd = pipd_ls_sp.cli:main\n"
124: 
125:     # RECORD covers every file except itself.
126:     record = "".join(_record_line(k, v) + "\n" for k, v in sorted(files.items()))
127:     record += f"{DISTINFO}/RECORD,,\n"
128:     files[f"{DISTINFO}/RECORD"] = record.encode()
129: 
130:     with zipfile.ZipFile(whl, "w", zipfile.ZIP_DEFLATED) as z:
131:         for k in sorted(files):
132:             zi = zipfile.ZipInfo(k, date_time=(1980, 1, 1, 0, 0, 0))   # deterministic archive
133:             zi.external_attr = 0o644 << 16
134:             z.writestr(zi, files[k])
135: 
136:     member_hashes = {k: _sha256(v) for k, v in sorted(files.items())}
137:     # a source bundle with the checksums a consumer can re-derive
138:     manifest = {
139:         "artefact": whl.name,
140:         "sha256": _sha256(whl.read_bytes()),
141:         "bytes": whl.stat().st_size,
142:         "members": member_hashes,
143:         "member_count": len(member_hashes),
144:         "product_digest": _sha256(_canonical_json([[k, member_hashes[k]] for k in sorted(member_hashes)]).encode()),
145:         "entry_points": {"pipd": "pipd_ls_sp.cli:main"},
146:         "requires_dist": ["jsonschema>=4.0"],
147:         "licence": "LicenseRef-PIPD-Proprietary",
148:         "built_by": "hand-built PEP 427 (no build backend available offline)",
149:         "candidate_head": _candidate_head(),
150:     }
151:     (DIST / "WHEEL_MANIFEST.json").write_text(json.dumps(manifest, ensure_ascii=False, indent=1),
152:                                               encoding="utf-8", newline="")
153:     sha = DIST / f"{whl.name}.sha256"
154:     sha.write_text(f"{manifest['sha256']}  {whl.name}\n", encoding="utf-8", newline="")
155:     return manifest
156: 
157: 
158: def main() -> int:
159:     m = build_wheel()
160:     print(json.dumps({k: m[k] for k in ("artefact", "sha256", "bytes", "member_count", "product_digest",
161:                                         "candidate_head", "entry_points", "licence")},
162:                      ensure_ascii=False, indent=1))
163:     return 0
164: 
165: 
166: if __name__ == "__main__":
167:     raise SystemExit(main())

## 2. What the maker changed since (verify, do not trust)

1. **C4 repair.** `tools/perf_budget.py` no longer counts obligations by `subject_id`. An obligation
   must carry a statement; its identity is sha256 of the normalised statement. Statement-less atoms
   are counted separately as `unvalidated_atom_count` and never vote; the rejected identity-addressed
   count and the rate it would have produced are printed beside the vote.
2. **C6 repair.** `README.md` line ~38 now names `r5p-post-challenge-repair` (baseline
   `r5-s4-packreader` @ `0f06eec9`).
3. **C8 definition fixed.** `ops/bound_digest.py` in `/round/ops/` publishes the ONE bound
   definition (`product_snapshot.walk` scope) and labels the narrower `equivalence.py` scope as
   rejected. `/round/evidence/DIGEST_BINDING.json` carries both numbers for `812af79`.
4. **C7 explanation.** The disputed case is also an existing in-repo test,
   `tests/test_negative_and_rollback.py::TestRollback::test_repair_subject_must_also_be_inside_the_authorised_root`,
   parameterised on `C:/Windows/system32/x.dll`. On Windows that spelling is absolute and outside the
   authorised root, so the guard fires; on Linux the same string is a *relative* path. The maker's
   host run of that test (Windows) passes. Judge for yourself whether the product is at fault, and
   say what the honest cross-platform claim is.
5. **C2 explanation.** The container has no `git` and mounts `/w` read-only, so git-dependent and
   write-dependent tests cannot pass there. The maker ran the suite on a clean Windows worktree of
   `812af79` (`/round/evidence/host_fullsuite_repair.txt`). **Do not re-run the full suite here** —
   instead state plainly whether the container is a valid suite environment at all, and require the
   host raw file as the suite evidence.

   or fall below the budget.** Also confirm the historical `context_bytes_per_artefact 343547 > 20000`
   FAIL row is still present and unrewritten.

   is under review, what the licence grants, and where acceptance lives.

   must carry a statement; its identity is sha256 of the normalised statement. Statement-less atoms
   are counted separately as `unvalidated_atom_count` and never vote; the rejected identity-addressed
   count and the rate it would have produced are printed beside the vote.
2. **C6 repair.** `README.md` line ~38 now names `r5p-post-challenge-repair` (baseline
   `r5-s4-packreader` @ `0f06eec9`).
3. **C8 definition fixed.** `ops/bound_digest.py` in `/round/ops/` publishes the ONE bound
   definition (`product_snapshot.walk` scope) and labels the narrower `equivalence.py` scope as
   rejected. `/round/evidence/DIGEST_BINDING.json` carries both numbers for `812af79`.
4. **C7 explanation.** The disputed case is also an existing in-repo test,
   `tests/test_negative_and_rollback.py::TestRollback::test_repair_subject_must_also_be_inside_the_authorised_root`,
   parameterised on `C:/Windows/system32/x.dll`. On Windows that spelling is absolute and outside the
   authorised root, so the guard fires; on Linux the same string is a *relative* path. The maker's
   host run of that test (Windows) passes. Judge for yourself whether the product is at fault, and
   say what the honest cross-platform claim is.
5. **C2 explanation.** The container has no `git` and mounts `/w` read-only, so git-dependent and
   write-dependent tests cannot pass there. The maker ran the suite on a clean Windows worktree of
   `812af79` (`/round/evidence/host_fullsuite_repair.txt`). **Do not re-run the full suite here** —
   instead state plainly whether the container is a valid suite environment at all, and require the
   host raw file as the suite evidence.

## 4. Rules

- Read-only on `/w`; write only under `/ao`. Never modify the product. Never use `git` state — there
  is none in `/w`; use file hashes.
- No credentials, no network, no `pip install` from an index; build the wheel from `/w` only.
- Every claim needs `command`, `exit`, raw stdout/stderr file, and the file/line it rests on.
- Distinguish `CONFIRMED_DEFECT`, `EVIDENCE_GAP`, `ORACLE_DISAGREEMENT` (your oracle was wrong),
  `NON_BLOCKING_OBSERVATION`, `OUT_OF_SCOPE`.
- Do not promote the claim ceiling. You may not issue `RELEASED`, `PRODUCTION_VERIFIED`, or accept on
  the maker's behalf.

25: def _repo_schemas_dir() -> Path:
26:     """Source-tree fallback: <repo_root>/schemas (keeps in-tree tests/tools working)."""
27:     return Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2] / "schemas"
28: 
29: 
30: def _packaged_schemas_dir() -> Path | None:
31:     """The schemas/ tree shipped INSIDE the installed package.
32: 
33:     Uses `importlib.resources.files("pipd_ls_sp") / "schemas"`. For a normal
34:     filesystem (unzipped wheel / editable) install `Path(...)` yields the real
35:     directory. For a zipped/re-homed install the resource is not a filesystem
36:     path, so it is materialised with `as_file` and copied to a persistent cache
37:     (the `as_file` temp dir is removed when its context exits). Returns the
38:     directory only when it actually contains `registry.json`; never assumes a
39:     filesystem path exists without checking it.
40:     """
41:     try:
42:         from importlib.resources import as_file, files
43:     except Exception:  # pragma: no cover - importlib.resources is stdlib on >=3.9
44:         return None
45:     try:
46:         resource = files("pipd_ls_sp") / "schemas"
47:     except Exception:  # pragma: no cover - package not importable as a resource
48:         return None
49:     try:
50:         direct = Path(resource)
51:     except TypeError:
52:         direct = None
53:     if direct is not None and (direct / "registry.json").is_file():
54:         return direct
55:     try:
56:         with as_file(resource) as materialized:
57:             mpath = Path(materialized)
58:             if not (mpath / "registry.json").is_file():
59:                 return None
60:             return _persist_tree(mpath)
61:     except Exception:  # pragma: no cover - unreadable/zipped edge
62:         return None
63: 
64: 
65: _PERSISTED: Path | None = None
66: 
67: 
68: def _persist_tree(src: Path) -> Path:
69:     """Copy a materialised (possibly ephemeral) schemas tree to a stable cache."""
70:     global _PERSISTED
71:     if _PERSISTED is not None and (_PERSISTED / "registry.json").is_file():
72:         return _PERSISTED
73:     import shutil
74:     import tempfile
75: 
76:     dst = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="pipd-schemas-")) / "schemas"
77:     shutil.copytree(src, dst)
78:     _PERSISTED = dst
79:     return dst
80: 
81: 
82: def _candidate_schemas_dirs_with_mode() -> list[tuple[str, Path]]:
83:     """Strict resolution order, tagged with the surface each candidate came from.
84: 
85:     ``OVERRIDE`` = ``$PIPD_SCHEMAS_DIR``; ``INSTALLED`` = the copy packaged inside the
86:     installed wheel; ``SOURCE`` = the ``<repo>/schemas`` source-tree fallback. This is the
87:     single owner of the order ``_resolve_schemas_dir`` consumes; it must never be reordered
88:     (``describe_schemas_source`` reports the SAME order to the operator).
89:     """
90:     candidates: list[tuple[str, Path]] = []
91:     override = os.environ.get("PIPD_SCHEMAS_DIR")
92:     if override:
93:         candidates.append(("OVERRIDE", Path(override)))
94:     packaged = _packaged_schemas_dir()
95:     if packaged is not None:
96:         candidates.append(("INSTALLED", packaged))
97:     candidates.append(("SOURCE", _repo_schemas_dir()))
98:     return candidates
99: 
100: 
101: def _candidate_schemas_dirs() -> list[Path]:
102:     """Strict resolution order: explicit override, packaged copy, source tree."""
103:     return [path for _, path in _candidate_schemas_dirs_with_mode()]
104: 
105: 
106: def _resolve_schemas_dir_and_mode() -> tuple[Path | None, str, list[str]]:
107:     """First candidate that contains registry.json, tagged with its surface mode."""
108:     tried: list[str] = []
109:     seen: set[str] = set()
110:     for mode, cand in _candidate_schemas_dirs_with_mode():
111:         key = str(cand)
112:         if key in seen:
113:             continue
114:         seen.add(key)
115:         tried.append(key)
116:         if (cand / "registry.json").is_file():
117:             return cand, mode, tried
118:     return None, "NONE", tried
119: 
120: 
121: def _resolve_schemas_dir() -> Path:
122:     """First location that contains registry.json, else typed ValidationFail."""
123:     resolved, _mode, tried = _resolve_schemas_dir_and_mode()
124:     if resolved is None:
125:         raise ValidationFail(
126:             "no schemas directory containing registry.json; tried: " + "; ".join(tried))
127:     return resolved
128: 
129: 
130: def describe_schemas_source() -> dict[str, Any]:
131:     """Public report of the schemas surface ``_resolve_schemas_dir`` actually selects.
132: 
133:     ``{"mode": "OVERRIDE"|"INSTALLED"|"SOURCE"|"NONE", "path": str, "tried": [str, ...]}``.
134:     ``OVERRIDE`` when ``$PIPD_SCHEMAS_DIR`` was selected, ``INSTALLED`` when the packaged
135:     copy was selected, ``SOURCE`` when the source-tree fallback was selected, and ``NONE``
136:     when nothing resolved (``path`` is then ``""``). ``tried`` lists the candidates in the
137:     order they were probed, so a caller can see exactly which surface a verdict came from
138:     instead of assuming a workspace-local ``schemas/`` convention. This never silently
139:     falls back to a different copy; it reports what the resolver will consume.
140:     """
141:     resolved, mode, tried = _resolve_schemas_dir_and_mode()
142:     return {"mode": mode, "path": str(resolved) if resolved is not None else "", "tried": tried}
143:

145: def load_registry(schemas_dir: Path | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]:
146:     d = schemas_dir or _resolve_schemas_dir()
147:     reg_path = d / "registry.json"
148:     if not reg_path.is_file():
149:         raise ValidationFail(f"registry missing: {reg_path}")
150:     data = json.loads(reg_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
151:     fams = data.get("families") or []
152:     if len(fams) != EXPECTED_FAMILIES:
153:         raise ValidationFail(f"registry family count {len(fams)} != {EXPECTED_FAMILIES}")
154:     names = [f["contract"] for f in fams]
155:     if names != SOURCE_ORDER:
156:         raise ValidationFail("registry family order/name diverges from §7.3 exact set")
157:     for f in fams:
158:         if not (d / f"{f['contract']}.schema.json").is_file():
159:             raise ValidationFail(f"schema file missing for {f['contract']}")
160:         if not f.get("required_fields"):
161:             raise ValidationFail(f"{f['contract']} has no required_fields")
162:     return data

167:     return json.loads((d / f"{contract}.schema.json").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))

92: def _explain(cmd: str, record: dict, extra: dict | None = None) -> dict:
93:     """A deterministic, human-readable derivation of a decision.
94: 
95:     Nothing here is model-generated: every line is derived from the record's own fields, so the
96:     explanation cannot drift from the artefact it explains.
97:     """
98:     why: list[str] = [f"command={cmd}", f"subject_id={record.get('subject_id', '?')}",
99:                       f"schema_version={record.get('schema_version', '?')}",
100:                       f"content_hash={str(record.get('content_hash', ''))[:16]}"]
101:     for key in ("profile", "verdict", "checked", "state", "scope", "writable_scope", "allowed_claims"):
102:         if key in record:
103:             why.append(f"{key}={json.dumps(record[key], ensure_ascii=False)}")
104:     if "findings" in record and record["findings"]:
105:         why.append(f"findings={len(record['findings'])}: "
106:                    + "; ".join(f.get("detail", "")[:60] for f in record["findings"][:3]))
107:     if extra:
108:         why += [f"{k}={json.dumps(v, ensure_ascii=False)[:120]}" for k, v in extra.items()]
109:     return {"explain": {"command": cmd, "derivation": why,
110:                         "generated_by": "deterministic field projection (no model consulted)"}}

1: """Workspace bootstrap, portable export, projection, doctor, diff, repair."""
2: from __future__ import annotations
3: 
4: import json
5: import re
6: from pathlib import Path
7: from typing import Any
8: 
9: from . import registry
10: from .errors import (DiffIncompatible, ExportSecretFound, ProjectionLoss,
11:                      RepairScopeFail, SelfAcceptForbidden, ToolDrift, ValidationFail)
12: from .util import canonical_json, sha256_file, sha256_text, utc_now

14: def _rx(*parts: str) -> "re.Pattern[str]":
15:     """Compile a secret pattern from fragments.
16: 
17:     Patterns are assembled from pieces on purpose: writing a credential-shaped literal into this
18:     file makes the environment's secret redactor rewrite it, which is how the bearer pattern was
19:     silently truncated (name replaced by a redaction marker, regex cut to an unmatchable stub).
20:     """
21:     return re.compile("".join(parts))
22: 
23: 
24: _GH = "git" + "hub_pat_"
25: _BEARER = "bea" + "rer"
26: _AUTHZ = "author" + "ization:"
27: _CLS = "[A-Za-z0-9"
28: _TAIL_CLS = _CLS + "\\-._~+/=]"
29: 
30: SECRET_PATTERNS = [
31:     ("github_pat_fine_grained", _rx(_GH, _CLS + "_]{20,}")),
32:     ("github_classic", _rx("gh", "[pousr]_", _CLS + "]{20,}")),
33:     ("openai_sk", _rx("sk-", _CLS + "]{20,}")),
34:     ("aws_access_key", _rx("AKI", "A[0-9A-Z]{16}")),
35:     ("private_key_block", _rx("-----BEGIN ", "[A-Z ]*PRIVATE KEY-----")),
36:     # (?i) <authz header> <space> <scheme> <space> >=20 token chars.
37:     # Token class widened to the RFC 6750 / base64 set: the original class omitted + and /,
38:     # so a standard-base64 bearer token went unmatched.
39:     ("bearer_literal", _rx("(?i)", _AUTHZ, "\\s*", _BEARER, "\\s+", _TAIL_CLS + "{20,}")),
40: ]
41: 
42: WEB_PACK_5 = ["index.html", "app.js", "styles.css", "manifest.webmanifest", "README.md"]
43: 
44: 
45: def init_workspace(root: Path) -> dict[str, Any]:
46:     created = []
47:     for rel in ("docs", "schemas", "src", "tests", "dist"):
48:         d = root / rel
49:         if not d.exists():
50:             d.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
51:             created.append(rel)
52:     reg = registry.load_registry(root / "schemas")
53:     return {"verdict": "PASS", "root": str(root), "created": created,
54:             "contract_families": len(reg["families"])}
55: 
56: 
57: def secret_scan(root: Path) -> dict[str, Any]:
58:     hits = []
59:     for p in sorted(root.rglob("*")):
60:         if not p.is_file() or ".git" in p.parts or p.suffix not in (".py", ".md", ".json", ".toml", ".yaml", ".yml", ".txt", ".sh"):
61:             continue
62:         if p.stat().st_size > 2_000_000:
63:             continue
64:         text = p.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
65:         for name, pat in SECRET_PATTERNS:
66:             for m in pat.finditer(text):
67:                 hits.append({"file": str(p.relative_to(root)), "kind": name,
68:                              "offset": m.start(), "redacted": True})
69:     return {"verdict": "PASS" if not hits else "FAIL", "hits": hits, "patterns": [n for n, _ in SECRET_PATTERNS]}
70: 
71: 
72: def export_manifest(root: Path, *, include: list[str] | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]:
73:     scan = secret_scan(root)
74:     if scan["verdict"] != "PASS":
75:         raise ExportSecretFound(f"secret scan found {len(scan['hits'])} hits")
76:     files = []
77:     for rel in include or ["schemas", "src", "tests", "docs", "pyproject.toml"]:
78:         target = root / rel
79:         if target.is_file():
80:             files.append(target)
81:         elif target.is_dir():
82:             files.extend(x for x in sorted(target.rglob("*")) if x.is_file()
83:                          and "__pycache__" not in x.parts and ".git" not in x.parts)
84:     rows = [{"rel": str(f.relative_to(root)).replace("\\", "/"),
85:              "size": f.stat().st_size, "sha256": sha256_file(f)} for f in files]
86:     rows.sort(key=lambda r: r["rel"])
87:     digest = sha256_text(canonical_json([[r["rel"], r["size"], r["sha256"]] for r in rows]))
88:     return {"schema": "PIPD-EXPORT-FREEZE/1", "generated_at": utc_now(),
89:             "file_count": len(rows), "manifest_sha256": digest, "files": rows,
90:             "secret_scan": scan["verdict"]}
91: 
92: 
93: def project_surfaces(root: Path, out, *, authorized_roots=None,
94:                      allow_replace=None) -> dict[str, Any]:
95:     """R-AUD-015 repair: delegate to the typed projection IR.
96: 
97:     This function previously wrote a hardcoded five-file site UI pack plus marker-only host stubs
98:     (`{"surface": "host:..."}`), i.e. exactly the empty payload the audit rejects: a non-empty JSON
99:     file was accepted as a successful load, and the Web denominator was satisfied by UI files. The
100:     typed IR implementation in `projection` is now the single source: it compiles every artifact
101:     from canonical objects + profile + adapter mapping, so a stub cannot be produced here, and the
102:     13-command CLI can no longer overwrite dist/web with a marker pack.
103: 
104:     R5-WO1 (REQ-PIPD-R5-DEST-001): the destination is resolved fail-closed and published by
105:     staging + atomic replace, so a caller-supplied `--out` can no longer delete an unintended tree.
106:     """
107:     from . import projection as _projection
108: 
109:     return _projection.project_surfaces(root, out, authorized_roots=authorized_roots,
110:                                         allow_replace=allow_replace)
111: 
112: def _check_schemas_surface(schemas_dir: Path, *, label: str) -> tuple[list[str], int | None]:
113:     """Validate ONE schemas surface end-to-end; return (findings, family_count).
114: 
115:     Shared by the workspace-local ``<root>/schemas`` check and the RESOLVED-surface check
116:     added for F-R5-01, so both surfaces are held to exactly the same bar: the registry-
117:     declared exact 19-family set/order is present, each ``<Family>.schema.json`` is present,
118:     parseable, on the 2020-12 draft, and enforces every registry-declared required field.
119:     Every finding NAMES the surface label, the family and the path. A ``None`` family count
120:     means the registry itself could not be loaded at that surface (a finding was emitted).
121:     """
122:     findings: list[str] = []
123:     try:
124:         reg = registry.load_registry(schemas_dir)
125:     except (ValidationFail, OSError, ValueError) as exc:
126:         return [f"{label}: registry not loadable at {schemas_dir}: {exc}"], None
127:     names = [f["contract"] for f in reg["families"]]
128:     if len(names) != len(set(names)):
129:         findings.append(f"{label}: duplicate contract family at {schemas_dir}")
130:     if names != registry.SOURCE_ORDER:
131:         findings.append(f"{label}: family set/order diverges from the exact 19 at {schemas_dir}: {names}")
132:     for f in reg["families"]:
133:         contract = f["contract"]
134:         schema_path = schemas_dir / f"{contract}.schema.json"
135:         try:
136:             schema = json.loads(schema_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
137:         except FileNotFoundError:
138:             findings.append(f"{label}: missing schema member {contract} at {schemas_dir} ({schema_path.name})")
139:             continue
140:         except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:
141:             findings.append(f"{label}: unreadable/unparseable schema member {contract} at {schemas_dir}: {exc}")
142:             continue
143:         if "2020-12" not in str(schema.get("$schema", "")):
144:             findings.append(f"{label}: {contract} schema draft is not 2020-12 at {schemas_dir}")
145:         enforced = set(schema.get("required", []))
146:         declared = set(f.get("required_fields", []))
147:         missing = declared - enforced
148:         if missing:
149:             findings.append(f"{label}: {contract} registry fields not enforced by schema "
150:                             f"{sorted(missing)} at {schemas_dir}")
151:     return findings, len(names)
152: 
153: 
154: def doctor(root: Path) -> dict[str, Any]:
155:     """Diagnose BOTH schemas surfaces and AND the verdict (F-R5-01 repair).
156: 
157:     Surface 1 is the workspace-local ``<root>/schemas`` tree (the pre-existing check).
158:     Surface 2 is the RESOLVED surface the CLI/registry actually consume, in order
159:     ``$PIPD_SCHEMAS_DIR`` -> packaged wheel copy -> ``<repo>/schemas`` fallback, as reported
160:     by ``registry.describe_schemas_source()``. The old implementation only ever read
161:     ``<root>/schemas``, so installing a wheel with a torn packaged ``schemas/`` copy and
162:     running ``pipd doctor`` from a healthy workspace printed PASS. The verdict is now the AND
163:     of both surfaces and is NEVER rescued by a different schemas copy: a failing resolved
164:     member names the family and the resolved path. ``schema_source`` tells the operator which
165:     surface was inspected (mode, path, tried).
166:     """
167:     findings: list[str] = []
168:     families: int | None = None
169: 
170:     local = root / "schemas"
171:     if local.is_dir():
172:         local_findings, local_families = _check_schemas_surface(local, label="workspace-local")
173:         findings.extend(local_findings)
174:         families = local_families
175: 
176:     source = registry.describe_schemas_source()
177:     resolved = Path(source["path"]) if source["path"] else None
178:     if resolved is None:
179:         findings.append("resolved schemas surface (mode=NONE): no schemas dir containing "
180:                         "registry.json was found; tried: " + "; ".join(source["tried"]))
181:     else:
182:         resolved_findings, resolved_families = _check_schemas_surface(
183:             resolved, label=f"resolved[{source['mode']}]")
184:         findings.extend(resolved_findings)
185:         if resolved_families is not None:
186:             families = resolved_families
187: 
188:     drift = scan_skill_drift(root)
189:     return {"verdict": "PASS" if not findings else "FAIL", "findings": findings,
190:             "families": families, "skill_drift": drift, "schema_source": source}

{"name": "C4_sources", "command": ["python3", "-c", "from pathlib import Path;p=Path(\"/w/tools/perf_budget.py\");print(\"\\n\".join(f\"{i}: {s}\" for i,s in enumerate(p.read_text().splitlines(),1)))"], "cwd": "/ao", "exit": 0, "stdout": "/ao/v2/raw/C4_sources.stdout", "stderr": "/ao/v2/raw/C4_sources.stderr"}
1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """S2 performance + context budget. Measures real wall-clock work; declares the budget it holds to.
3: 
4: Every threshold now carries its PROVENANCE: `measurement` (how the value is produced),
5: `denominator` (what the value is divided by, or "" when it is an absolute bound) and
6: `source_of_truth` (the file/rule the number is derived from). A threshold with no such rule in the
7: repo is marked `UNPROVENANCED`. The verdict is **fail-closed**: an *exceeded* budget is always
8: `FAIL` (provenance cannot turn an over-budget measurement green), a within-budget unprovenanced row
9: is `UNDECIDABLE` (never PASS), and a within-budget provenanced row is `PASS`. `--check` exits
10: non-zero whenever any row FAILs. No numeric threshold was raised or lowered by adding this
11: provenance.
12: 
13: WO-S4-CAL-003 (F-R5-04) adds an ANTI-DILUTION oracle on top of that provenance, without touching any
14: threshold or the owner's ruling. The voting rate is computed against a UNIQUE validated obligation
15: denominator (distinct obligation identities), not the raw length of the atom list, so appending
16: duplicate/filler atoms cannot dilute `bytes_per_atom` at or below its budget. The raw `atoms` count is
17: still reported beside `unique_obligations` (with an explicit `dedup_rule`). Serialized/disk bytes and
18: the bytes that would actually enter a model context are kept SEPARATE: the latter is not computable
19: from the product and is reported `UNPROVENANCED`, never invented.
20: """
21: from __future__ import annotations
22: 
23: import argparse
24: import copy
25: import json
26: import math
27: import subprocess
28: import sys
29: import time
30: from pathlib import Path
31: 
32: ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
33: sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / "src"))
34: OUT = ROOT / ".hgk" / "artifacts"
35: 
36: # The numeric thresholds are unchanged; provenance is added alongside them, never folded into them.
37: #
38: # R5 S4 owner adjudication (2026-10-10): the four original thresholds were UNPROVENANCED and the
39: # absolute `context_bytes_per_artefact` row could never pass on a corpus of this size. The owner
40: # re-specified the budget as a scale-invariant RATE (`bytes_per_atom`) and demoted the original
41: # absolute row plus the three timing rows to ADVISORY: they stay measured, stay printed, and keep
42: # their historical verdicts, but they no longer vote on the overall verdict. The historical
43: # block reports the RECORDED value that the frozen BYTES_PER_ATOM_BASELINE.json supplies for a
44: # metric (`pi_bytes` = 343,547 for `context_bytes_per_artefact`), so the 343,547 > 20,000 FAIL is
45: # host-stable; the live reading is kept beside it as `measured_now`, and every row marks whether
46: # its `value` is `recorded`.
47: # No threshold was raised or lowered by this change; only which rows vote changed.
48: PROVENANCE = {
49:     "compile_chain_ms": {
50:         "budget": 2000,
51:         "measurement": "wall-clock perf_counter around intake->compile_pi->bind_pd->"
52:                        "compile_construction_contract->compile_ecp->compile_tqaep, ms",
53:         "denominator": "",
54:         "source_of_truth": "UNPROVENANCED",
55:         "voting": False,
56:         "advisory_reason": "no consumer was ever named for this bound; measured headroom 2.35x, "
57:                            "and no entity in the product depends on the number",
58:     },
59:     "validate_19_contracts_ms": {
60:         "budget": 3000,
61:         "measurement": "wall-clock perf_counter loading every registry family schema, ms",
62:         "denominator": "19 registry families (registry.json)",
63:         "source_of_truth": "UNPROVENANCED",
64:         "voting": False,
65:         "advisory_reason": "measured headroom 1,579x - the row cannot fire and carries no signal",
66:     },
67:     "cli_cold_start_ms": {
68:         "budget": 6000,
69:         "measurement": "wall-clock perf_counter of `python -B -m pipd_ls_sp.cli --help` "
70:                        "in a fresh interpreter, ms",
71:         "denominator": "",
72:         "source_of_truth": "UNPROVENANCED",
73:         "voting": False,
74:         "advisory_reason": "measured headroom 51x - the row cannot fire and carries no signal",
75:     },
76:     "context_bytes_per_artefact": {
77:         "budget": 20000,
78:         "measurement": "max of len(json.dumps(artefact)) in UTF-8 bytes over PI/PD/CC/ECP/TQAEP",
79:         "denominator": "one artefact (bytes per artefact, not per atom)",
80:         "source_of_truth": "UNPROVENANCED",
81:         "voting": False,
82:         "advisory_reason": "superseded by the owner re-specification of 2026-10-10: the budget is "
83:                            "now the scale-invariant rate bytes_per_atom. The absolute reading is "
84:                            "kept because its historical FAIL must stay visible, not to decide.",
85:         "preserve_history": True,
86:     },
87:     # The voting row, per docs/OWNER_ADJUDICATION_R5_S4_2026-10-10.json
88:     # ruling S2_BUDGET_DEFINITION = PER_ATOM_BYTES(2000).
89:     "bytes_per_atom": {
90:         "budget": 2000,
91:         "measurement": "len(json.dumps(PI, ensure_ascii=False)) UTF-8 bytes divided by the number "
92:                        "of DISTINCT obligation identities in PI.stable_semantic_contract.atoms "
93:                        "(padding-invariant denominator; raw atom count reported as `atoms`)",
94:         "denominator": "one distinct obligation (bytes per unique obligation, scale-invariant)",
95:         "source_of_truth": "docs/OWNER_ADJUDICATION_R5_S4_2026-10-10.json#/rulings/S2_BUDGET_DEFINITION",
96:         "voting": True,
97:     },
98:     # WO-S4-CAL-003: the 95th-percentile / max per-atom statistics are modelling aids ONLY. They are
99:     # reported so a long tail is visible, but they are UNPROVENANCED and do NOT vote - they must never
100:     # silently replace the owner's ratified MEAN-based `bytes_per_atom` row.
101:     "per_atom_p95_bytes": {
102:         "budget": 2000,
103:         "measurement": "nearest-rank 95th percentile of len(json.dumps(atom)) UTF-8 bytes over PI atoms",
104:         "denominator": "one atom (95th-percentile atom, bytes)",
105:         "source_of_truth": "UNPROVENANCED",
106:         "voting": False,
107:         "advisory_reason": "the owner's ratified row is the MEAN bytes_per_atom; the p95/max tail "
108:                            "statistics are reported for visibility and MUST NOT replace it",
109:     },
110:     "per_atom_max_bytes": {
111:         "budget": 2000,
112:         "measurement": "max of len(json.dumps(atom)) UTF-8 bytes over PI atoms",
113:         "denominator": "one atom (largest atom, bytes)",
114:         "source_of_truth": "UNPROVENANCED",
115:         "voting": False,
116:         "advisory_reason": "the owner's ratified row is the MEAN bytes_per_atom; the max tail "
117:                            "statistic is reported for visibility and MUST NOT replace it",
118:     },
119: }
120: BUDGET = {k: v["budget"] for k, v in PROVENANCE.items()}
121: 
122: # Regression guard (R5 of the FAR): the rate must not grow against a frozen baseline. The baseline
123: # is only written by an explicit --freeze-baseline, so --check never mutates the tree.
124: BASELINE_FILE = "BYTES_PER_ATOM_BASELINE.json"
125: REGRESSION_TOLERANCE_PCT = 10.0
126: 
127: # Advisory (historical) metrics whose `value` must come from a FROZEN recording, not from a fresh
128: # measurement on this host: a recorded verdict that moves with the machine is not a record. Only
129: # metrics listed here are recorded; everything else keeps its live measurement (recorded: false).
130: RECORDED_BASELINE = {
131:     "context_bytes_per_artefact": {
132:         "file": Path(".hgk", "artifacts", "s2", BASELINE_FILE).as_posix(),
133:         "field": "pi_bytes",
134:     },
135: }
136: 
137: # WO-S4-CAL-003: the anti-dilution rule, strengthened after independent challenge C4 (2026-10-10).
138: # An obligation's identity is the CONTENT it asserts - its normalised statement text, else its canonical
139: # JSON without the identity trio. Two atoms that assert the same thing are ONE obligation no matter what
140: # `subject_id`/`req_id` they carry, so neither duplicated filler nor re-stamped filler can inflate the
141: # divisor. The weaker identity-addressed count is still reported beside the voting one.
142: DEDUP_RULE = ("distinct VALIDATED obligation: an atom only counts as an obligation when it carries a "
143:               "statement, and its identity is sha256 of that statement normalised (whitespace collapsed, "
144:               "case-folded). Atoms with no statement are never obligations: they are reported separately "
145:               "as `unvalidated_atom_count` and can inflate neither the divisor nor the rate. The weaker "
146:               "identity-addressed count (subject_id first) is also reported, never voted on.")
147: _IDENTITY_KEYS = ("subject_id", "version", "content_hash", "schema_version")
148: _STATEMENT_KEYS = ("source_clause", "text", "statement", "requirement", "req_text")
149: 
150: 
151: def _canonical_json(value) -> str:
152:     """Canonical JSON (sorted keys, no whitespace) - the same shape pipeline.util.canonical_json
153:     produces, defined locally so this module imports no product code at import time."""
154:     return json.dumps(value, ensure_ascii=False, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"))
155: 
156: 
157: def obligation_statement(atom: dict) -> str:
158:     """The atom's obligation statement, whitespace/case-normalised; '' when it carries none.
159: 
160:     Only declared statement fields are read - never free-form body fields - so a filler atom cannot
161:     become an obligation by being given a `note`.
162:     """
163:     parts: list[str] = []
164: 
165:     def _collect(value) -> None:
166:         if isinstance(value, str):
167:             parts.append(value)
168:         elif isinstance(value, dict):
169:             for k in sorted(value):
170:                 _collect(value[k])
171:         elif isinstance(value, list):
172:             for v in value:
173:                 _collect(v)
174: 
175:     for key in _STATEMENT_KEYS:
176:         if key in atom:
177:             _collect(atom[key])
178:     return " ".join(" ".join(parts).split()).lower()
179: 
180: 
181: def canonical_identity(atom: dict) -> str:
182:     """Content-addressed identity of one VALIDATED obligation, or '' when the atom asserts nothing.
183: 
184:     Statement text is the identity. Re-stamping `subject_id`/`req_id` on identical filler does not
185:     create a new obligation; neither does adding body fields to a statement-less atom.
186:     """
187:     text = obligation_statement(atom)
188:     return ("text:" + _sha256_hex(text)) if text else ""
189: 
190: 
191: def identity_addressed_count(atoms: list[dict]) -> int:
192:     """The weaker (rejected) denominator: subject_id first, else the atom body.
193: 
194:     Kept only so the round can show, in its own output, that this rule is gameable by re-stamped
195:     filler. It never votes.
196:     """
197:     ids = set()
198:     for a in atoms:
199:         sid = a.get("subject_id")
200:         if isinstance(sid, str) and sid:
201:             ids.add("subject_id:" + sid)
202:         else:
203:             body = {k: v for k, v in a.items() if k not in _IDENTITY_KEYS}
204:             ids.add("canon-json:" + _sha256_hex(_canonical_json(body)))
205:     return len(ids)
206: 
207: 
208: def _sha256_hex(text: str) -> str:
209:     import hashlib
210:     return hashlib.sha256(text.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
211: 
212: 
213: def unique_obligations(atoms: list[dict]) -> int:
214:     """Count DISTINCT VALIDATED obligations (the defensible denominator)."""
215:     return len({i for i in (canonical_identity(a) for a in atoms) if i})
216: 
217: 
218: def unvalidated_atom_count(atoms: list[dict]) -> int:
219:     """Atoms that assert no statement at all: padding, never obligations."""
220:     return sum(1 for a in atoms if not obligation_statement(a))
221: 
222: 
223: def _nearest_rank(values: list[int], pct: float) -> float:
224:     """Nearest-rank percentile (no interpolation): a real atom's size, never a synthetic value."""
225:     if not values:
226:         return 0.0
227:     ordered = sorted(values)
228:     rank = math.ceil(pct / 100.0 * len(ordered))
229:     return float(ordered[max(rank, 1) - 1])
230: 
231: 
232: def per_atom_profile(pi: dict) -> dict:
233:     """The voting rate plus its padding-invariance inputs and the tail statistics.
234: 
235:     `bytes_per_atom` divides the serialized PI bytes by the number of DISTINCT obligations, so
236:     padding atoms cannot dilute it. A doc with zero obligations yields an infinite rate (fail-closed).
237:     """
238:     sc = pi.get("stable_semantic_contract") or {}
239:     sc_wo = {k: v for k, v in sc.items() if k != "atoms"}
240:     pi_bytes = len(json.dumps(pi, ensure_ascii=False).encode())
241:     container = len(json.dumps(sc_wo, ensure_ascii=False).encode())
242:     atoms = sc.get("atoms") or []
243:     sizes = [len(json.dumps(a, ensure_ascii=False).encode()) for a in atoms]
244:     uniq = unique_obligations(atoms)
245:     by_id = identity_addressed_count(atoms)
246:     unvalidated = unvalidated_atom_count(atoms)
247:     rate = pi_bytes / uniq if uniq else float("inf")
248:     return {"pi_bytes": pi_bytes, "bytes": pi_bytes, "atoms": len(atoms),
249:             "unique_obligations": uniq, "unique_obligations_by_identity": by_id,
250:             "unvalidated_atom_count": unvalidated,
251:             "dedup_rule": DEDUP_RULE,
252:             "bytes_per_atom": rate, "per_atom_sizes": sizes,
253:             "p95_bytes": _nearest_rank(sizes, 95.0),
254:             "max_bytes": float(max(sizes)) if sizes else 0.0}
255: 
256: 
257: # WO-S4-CAL-003 scale fixtures. A "single long clause" is one syntactically valid clause with no
258: # clause/conjunction boundaries, so it compiles to exactly ONE atom (a genuine long tail for the
259: # max/p95 statistics). The filler strings below are punctuation-free so the requirement compiler
260: # cannot split them; they are used ONLY to build in-memory fixtures, never written to the tree.
261: _LONG_CLAUSE_FILLER = "編譯器需將每個來源條款轉換成獨立原子需求同時保存來源定位極性並提供可重現摘要識別"
262: _FILLER_ATOM = {"req_id": "REQ-FILLER", "owner": "PIPD-EC", "axis": "intent"}
263: 
264: # The unique obligation identities the three fixtures exercise.
265: SCALE_UNIQUE_OBLIGATIONS = (2, 237, 801)
266: 
267: 
268: def long_clause_scale_fixture(repeats: int = 60) -> dict:
269:     """One syntactically valid multi-thousand-character clause -> exactly one atom."""
270:     from pipd_ls_sp import requirements as req
271:     clause = _LONG_CLAUSE_FILLER * repeats
272:     atoms = req.compile_requirements(clause, ["."])
273:     pi = {"subject_id": "PI-SCALE-LONG", "version": "1", "content_hash": "x" * 64,
274:           "schema_version": "PI-PKG@1", "trace": [{"from_id": "PI-SCALE-LONG"}],
275:           "stable_semantic_contract": {"goal": clause[:200], "atoms": atoms}}
276:     return {"clause_chars": len(clause), "profile": per_atom_profile(pi)}
277: 
278: 
279: def _scale_fixture(n_unique: int) -> dict:
280:     """A synthetic PI with exactly `n_unique` DISTINCT obligations and no duplicates."""
281:     atoms = []
282:     for i in range(n_unique):
283:         a = dict(_FILLER_ATOM)
284:         a["subject_id"] = f"ATOM-SCALE-{i}"
285:         a["req_id"] = f"REQ-SCALE-{i}"
286:         # Each of the n_unique atoms asserts a DIFFERENT statement, which is what makes it a distinct
287:         # validated obligation. A statement-less atom is filler and would not count at all.
288:         a["source_clause"] = {"file": "fixtures/scale.md", "clause_id": f"clause-{i}",
289:                               "text": f"obligation {i}: the service MUST satisfy requirement {i}"}
290:         atoms.append(a)
291:     return {"subject_id": "PI-SCALE", "version": "1", "content_hash": "y" * 64,
292:             "schema_version": "PI-PKG@1", "stable_semantic_contract": {"goal": "scale", "atoms": atoms}}
293: 
294: 
295: def scale_fixtures() -> dict:
296:     """Exercise 2 / 237 / 801 unique obligations and one genuinely long single clause."""
297:     out: dict = {}
298:     for n in SCALE_UNIQUE_OBLIGATIONS:
299:         prof = per_atom_profile(_scale_fixture(n))
300:         out[str(n)] = {"atoms": prof["atoms"], "unique_obligations": prof["unique_obligations"],
301:                        "pi_bytes": prof["pi_bytes"], "bytes_per_atom": round(prof["bytes_per_atom"], 2),
302:                        "p95_bytes": prof["p95_bytes"], "max_bytes": prof["max_bytes"]}
303:     long_fx = long_clause_scale_fixture()
304:     prof = long_fx["profile"]
305:     out["long_clause"] = {"clause_chars": long_fx["clause_chars"], "atoms": prof["atoms"],
306:                           "unique_obligations": prof["unique_obligations"],
307:                           "pi_bytes": prof["pi_bytes"],
308:                           "bytes_per_atom": round(prof["bytes_per_atom"], 2),
309:                           "p95_bytes": prof["p95_bytes"], "max_bytes": prof["max_bytes"]}
310:     return out
311: 
312: 
313: def padding_invariance_probe(padding: int = 500) -> dict:
314:     """Appending `padding` duplicated/filler atoms must NOT bring the rate at or below budget.
315: 
316:     This is a real assertion over the module's own `per_atom_profile`, not a comment: it builds an
317:     over-budget single-obligation payload, pads it with `padding` copies of a filler atom, and
318:     asserts the padded rate is still > the budget. If the denominator were the raw atom count a
319:     padding of this size would trivially dilute it below 2000.
320:     """
321:     long_clause = _LONG_CLAUSE_FILLER * 60
322:     atoms = [{"subject_id": "ATOM-LONG", "req_id": "REQ-LONG",
323:               "source_clause": {"text": long_clause}}]
324:     pi = {"subject_id": "PI-PAD", "version": "1", "content_hash": "z" * 64,
325:           "schema_version": "PI-PKG@1",
326:           "stable_semantic_contract": {"goal": "pad", "atoms": atoms}}
327:     before = per_atom_profile(pi)
328:     padded = copy.deepcopy(pi)
329:     filler = {"subject_id": "ATOM-LONG", "req_id": "REQ-LONG", "note": "padding"}
330:     padded["stable_semantic_contract"]["atoms"] = atoms + [dict(filler) for _ in range(padding)]
331:     after = per_atom_profile(padded)
332:     budget = PROVENANCE["bytes_per_atom"]["budget"]
333:     naive = after["pi_bytes"] / after["atoms"] if after["atoms"] else float("inf")
334:     ok = after["bytes_per_atom"] > budget and after["unique_obligations"] == before["unique_obligations"]
335:     assert ok, (f"padding changed the denominator: before={before['bytes_per_atom']:.1f} "
336:                 f"after={after['bytes_per_atom']:.1f} budget={budget}")
337:     assert naive <= budget, "the fixture must be one that WOULD dilute a raw atom-count denominator"
338: 
339:     # Independent challenge C4 (2026-10-10) found the weaker attack: keep the statement identical but
340:     # re-stamp each filler atom with a FRESH subject_id/req_id, which defeated the identity-addressed
341:     # denominator. The content-addressed rule must survive it; the forged variant is measured too, and
342:     # its identity-addressed rate is reported so the weaker rule's failure stays visible.
343:     forged = copy.deepcopy(pi)
344:     forged_atoms = list(atoms) + [
345:         {"subject_id": f"ATOM-FORGED-{i:04d}", "req_id": f"REQ-FORGED-{i:04d}",
346:          "note": "padding"} for i in range(padding)]
347:     forged["stable_semantic_contract"]["atoms"] = forged_atoms
348:     after_forged = per_atom_profile(forged)
349:     naive_forged = (after_forged["pi_bytes"] / after_forged["unique_obligations_by_identity"]
350:                     if after_forged["unique_obligations_by_identity"] else float("inf"))
351:     ok_forged = (after_forged["bytes_per_atom"] > budget
352:                  and after_forged["unique_obligations"] == before["unique_obligations"])
353:     assert ok_forged, (f"re-stamped filler changed the denominator: "
354:                        f"unique={after_forged['unique_obligations']} "
355:                        f"rate={after_forged['bytes_per_atom']:.1f} budget={budget}")
356:     assert naive_forged <= budget, ("the forged-identity fixture must be one that WOULD dilute an "
357:                                     "identity-addressed denominator")
358: 
359:     return {"before": {"atoms": before["atoms"], "unique_obligations": before["unique_obligations"],
360:                        "bytes_per_atom": round(before["bytes_per_atom"], 2)},
361:             "after": {"atoms": after["atoms"], "unique_obligations": after["unique_obligations"],
362:                       "bytes_per_atom": round(after["bytes_per_atom"], 2)},
363:             "after_restamped_ids": {
364:                 "atoms": after_forged["atoms"],
365:                 "unique_obligations": after_forged["unique_obligations"],
366:                 "unique_obligations_by_identity": after_forged["unique_obligations_by_identity"],
367:                 "bytes_per_atom": round(after_forged["bytes_per_atom"], 2),
368:                 "rate_if_denominator_were_identity_addressed": round(naive_forged, 2)},
369:             "padding_atoms": padding, "budget": budget,
370:             "naive_rate_if_denominator_were_raw_atoms": round(naive, 2),
371:             "padding_invariance": "PASS",
372:             "restamped_identity_invariance": "PASS"}
373: 
374: 
375: def _verdict(value: float, budget: float, source_of_truth: str) -> str:
376:     """Decide one threshold's verdict, fail-closed.
377: 
378:     An *exceeded* budget is always FAIL - an over-budget measurement is a fact about the artefact,
379:     not a statement about whether someone ratified the threshold. Provenance can only downgrade a
380:     within-budget row (PASS -> UNDECIDABLE); it can never turn an over-budget row green.
381:     """
382:     if value > budget:
383:         return "FAIL"
384:     if source_of_truth == "UNPROVENANCED":
385:         return "UNDECIDABLE"
386:     return "PASS"
387: 
388: 
389: UNRATIFIED_NOTE = ("threshold authority is unratified: no in-repo source_of_truth; the row is "
390:                    "decided by the measurement alone (an over-budget value still FAILs)")
391: 
392: 
393: def _row(metric: str, value: float, **extra) -> dict:
394:     prov = PROVENANCE[metric]
395:     # The decision uses the UNROUNDED raw measurement; rounding is display-only. Rounding first
396:     # could hide a real overrun (e.g. raw 2000.04 against a 2000 budget rounds to 2000.0 and would
397:     # read as within budget). `value`/`value_display` are what gets printed.
398:     display = round(value, 1) if isinstance(value, float) else value
399:     voting = extra.pop("voting", None)
400:     if voting is None:
401:         voting = prov.get("voting", True)
402:     row = {"metric": metric, "value": display, "value_display": display,
403:            "budget": prov["budget"], **extra,
404:            "measurement": prov["measurement"], "denominator": prov["denominator"],
405:            "source_of_truth": prov["source_of_truth"],
406:            "voting": voting}
407:     if prov.get("uncomputable"):
408:         row["uncomputable"] = True
409:         row["computed"] = False
410:     if "advisory_reason" in prov:
411:         row["advisory"] = True
412:         row["advisory_reason"] = prov["advisory_reason"]
413:     if prov.get("preserve_history"):
414:         row["preserved"] = True
415:     row["exceeded"] = value > prov["budget"]
416:     row["verdict"] = _verdict(value, prov["budget"], prov["source_of_truth"])
417:     if prov["source_of_truth"] == "UNPROVENANCED":
418:         row["note"] = UNRATIFIED_NOTE
419:     return row
420: 
421: 
422: def overall_verdict(rows: list[dict]) -> str:
423:     """Overall verdict: any FAIL dominates; UNDECIDABLE only when nothing failed."""
424:     if any(r.get("verdict") == "FAIL" for r in rows):
425:         return "FAIL"
426:     if any(r.get("verdict") == "UNDECIDABLE" for r in rows):
427:         return "UNDECIDABLE"
428:     return "PASS"
429: 
430: 
431: def exit_code(verdict: str) -> int:
432:     """Non-zero iff the overall verdict is FAIL; UNDECIDABLE without any exceeded row is not a
433:     hard failure."""
434:     return 1 if verdict == "FAIL" else 0
435: 
436: 
437: def _recorded_value(metric: str, baseline: dict | None) -> tuple[object, dict | None]:
438:     """The RECORDED value for an advisory metric, read from a frozen baseline.
439: 
440:     Returns `(value, origin)` when a recorded baseline supplies the metric, else `(None, None)`.
441:     Never invents a number: an absent, unreadable, or field-less baseline yields `(None, None)`
442:     and the caller must fall back to the live measurement *and say so*.
443:     """
444:     spec = RECORDED_BASELINE.get(metric)
445:     if not spec or not baseline or spec["field"] not in baseline:
446:         return None, None
447:     return baseline[spec["field"]], {"file": spec["file"], "field": spec["field"]}
448: 
449: 
450: def _historical_row(row: dict, baseline: dict | None) -> dict:
451:     """One `historical` row: the RECORDED value when the baseline supplies one, else the live
452:     measurement (marked `recorded: false` with a note, never a fabricated record)."""
453:     metric = row["metric"]
454:     measured_now = row["value"]
455:     recorded, origin = _recorded_value(metric, baseline)
456:     value = measured_now if recorded is None else recorded
457:     out = {"metric": metric, "value": value, "measured_now": measured_now,
458:            "recorded": recorded is not None, "origin": origin,
459:            "budget": row["budget"],
460:            "source_of_truth": row["source_of_truth"]}
461:     if value is None:
462:         # WO-S4-CAL-003: an UNPROVENANCED quantity the product cannot compute. Never score it: a
463:         # null is not "under budget", so there is no PASS/FAIL to read out of it.
464:         out.update({"verdict": "UNPROVENANCED", "exceeded": None, "computed": False,
465:                     "note": "uncomputable from the product (see measurement); no value is invented"})
466:     else:
467:         out.update({"verdict": _verdict(value, row["budget"], row["source_of_truth"]),
468:                     "exceeded": value > row["budget"]})
469:         if recorded is None:
470:             out["note"] = ("no recorded value was available for this metric; `value` is the live "
471:                            "measurement, not a recorded one")
472:     return out
473: 
474: 
475: def candidate_head(repo: Path | None = None) -> str:
476:     """Resolve HEAD via `git -C <repo> rev-parse HEAD`; honest fallback only when git is absent."""
477:     repo = repo or ROOT
478:     try:
479:         proc = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", str(repo), "rev-parse", "HEAD"],
480:                               capture_output=True, text=True)
481:     except (FileNotFoundError, OSError):
482:         return "UNBOUND_NO_GIT_BINARY"
483:     if proc.returncode != 0:
484:         return "UNBOUND_NO_GIT_BINARY"
485:     head = proc.stdout.strip()
486:     return head or "UNBOUND_NO_GIT_BINARY"
487: 
488: 
489: def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
490:     ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
491:     ap.add_argument("--check", action="store_true",
492:                     help="report each budget entry's verdict; UNDECIDABLE is not a failure")
493:     ap.add_argument("--freeze-baseline", action="store_true",
494:                     help="record the current bytes_per_atom as the regression baseline (the only "
495:                          "mode that writes anything)")
496:     args = ap.parse_args(argv)
497: 
498:     from pipd_ls_sp import pipeline as P, validate as V

137: # WO-S4-CAL-003: the anti-dilution rule, strengthened after independent challenge C4 (2026-10-10).
138: # An obligation's identity is the CONTENT it asserts - its normalised statement text, else its canonical
139: # JSON without the identity trio. Two atoms that assert the same thing are ONE obligation no matter what
140: # `subject_id`/`req_id` they carry, so neither duplicated filler nor re-stamped filler can inflate the
141: # divisor. The weaker identity-addressed count is still reported beside the voting one.
142: DEDUP_RULE = ("distinct VALIDATED obligation: an atom only counts as an obligation when it carries a "
143:               "statement, and its identity is sha256 of that statement normalised (whitespace collapsed, "
144:               "case-folded). Atoms with no statement are never obligations: they are reported separately "
145:               "as `unvalidated_atom_count` and can inflate neither the divisor nor the rate. The weaker "
146:               "identity-addressed count (subject_id first) is also reported, never voted on.")
147: _IDENTITY_KEYS = ("subject_id", "version", "content_hash", "schema_version")
148: _STATEMENT_KEYS = ("source_clause", "text", "statement", "requirement", "req_text")
149: 
150: 
151: def _canonical_json(value) -> str:
152:     """Canonical JSON (sorted keys, no whitespace) - the same shape pipeline.util.canonical_json
153:     produces, defined locally so this module imports no product code at import time."""
154:     return json.dumps(value, ensure_ascii=False, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"))
155: 
156: 
157: def obligation_statement(atom: dict) -> str:
158:     """The atom's obligation statement, whitespace/case-normalised; '' when it carries none.
159: 
160:     Only declared statement fields are read - never free-form body fields - so a filler atom cannot
161:     become an obligation by being given a `note`.
162:     """
163:     parts: list[str] = []
164: 
165:     def _collect(value) -> None:
166:         if isinstance(value, str):
167:             parts.append(value)
168:         elif isinstance(value, dict):
169:             for k in sorted(value):
170:                 _collect(value[k])
171:         elif isinstance(value, list):
172:             for v in value:
173:                 _collect(v)
174: 
175:     for key in _STATEMENT_KEYS:
176:         if key in atom:
177:             _collect(atom[key])
178:     return " ".join(" ".join(parts).split()).lower()
179: 
180: 
181: def canonical_identity(atom: dict) -> str:
182:     """Content-addressed identity of one VALIDATED obligation, or '' when the atom asserts nothing.
183: 
184:     Statement text is the identity. Re-stamping `subject_id`/`req_id` on identical filler does not
185:     create a new obligation; neither does adding body fields to a statement-less atom.
186:     """
187:     text = obligation_statement(atom)
188:     return ("text:" + _sha256_hex(text)) if text else ""
189: 
190: 
191: def identity_addressed_count(atoms: list[dict]) -> int:
192:     """The weaker (rejected) denominator: subject_id first, else the atom body.
193: 
194:     Kept only so the round can show, in its own output, that this rule is gameable by re-stamped
195:     filler. It never votes.
196:     """
197:     ids = set()
198:     for a in atoms:
199:         sid = a.get("subject_id")
200:         if isinstance(sid, str) and sid:
201:             ids.add("subject_id:" + sid)
202:         else:
203:             body = {k: v for k, v in a.items() if k not in _IDENTITY_KEYS}
204:             ids.add("canon-json:" + _sha256_hex(_canonical_json(body)))
205:     return len(ids)
206: 
207: 
208: def _sha256_hex(text: str) -> str:
209:     import hashlib
210:     return hashlib.sha256(text.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
211: 
212: 
213: def unique_obligations(atoms: list[dict]) -> int:
214:     """Count DISTINCT VALIDATED obligations (the defensible denominator)."""
215:     return len({i for i in (canonical_identity(a) for a in atoms) if i})
216: 
217: 
218: def unvalidated_atom_count(atoms: list[dict]) -> int:
219:     """Atoms that assert no statement at all: padding, never obligations."""
220:     return sum(1 for a in atoms if not obligation_statement(a))
221: 
222: 
223: def _nearest_rank(values: list[int], pct: float) -> float:
224:     """Nearest-rank percentile (no interpolation): a real atom's size, never a synthetic value."""
225:     if not values:
226:         return 0.0
227:     ordered = sorted(values)
228:     rank = math.ceil(pct / 100.0 * len(ordered))
229:     return float(ordered[max(rank, 1) - 1])
230: 
231: 
232: def per_atom_profile(pi: dict) -> dict:
233:     """The voting rate plus its padding-invariance inputs and the tail statistics.
234: 
235:     `bytes_per_atom` divides the serialized PI bytes by the number of DISTINCT obligations, so
236:     padding atoms cannot dilute it. A doc with zero obligations yields an infinite rate (fail-closed).
237:     """
238:     sc = pi.get("stable_semantic_contract") or {}
239:     sc_wo = {k: v for k, v in sc.items() if k != "atoms"}
240:     pi_bytes = len(json.dumps(pi, ensure_ascii=False).encode())
241:     container = len(json.dumps(sc_wo, ensure_ascii=False).encode())
242:     atoms = sc.get("atoms") or []
243:     sizes = [len(json.dumps(a, ensure_ascii=False).encode()) for a in atoms]
244:     uniq = unique_obligations(atoms)
245:     by_id = identity_addressed_count(atoms)
246:     unvalidated = unvalidated_atom_count(atoms)
247:     rate = pi_bytes / uniq if uniq else float("inf")
248:     return {"pi_bytes": pi_bytes, "bytes": pi_bytes, "atoms": len(atoms),
249:             "unique_obligations": uniq, "unique_obligations_by_identity": by_id,
250:             "unvalidated_atom_count": unvalidated,
251:             "dedup_rule": DEDUP_RULE,
252:             "bytes_per_atom": rate, "per_atom_sizes": sizes,
253:             "p95_bytes": _nearest_rank(sizes, 95.0),
254:             "max_bytes": float(max(sizes)) if sizes else 0.0}
255: 
256: 
257: # WO-S4-CAL-003 scale fixtures. A "single long clause" is one syntactically valid clause with no
258: # clause/conjunction boundaries, so it compiles to exactly ONE atom (a genuine long tail for the
259: # max/p95 statistics). The filler strings below are punctuation-free so the requirement compiler
260: # cannot split them; they are used ONLY to build in-memory fixtures, never written to the tree.
261: _LONG_CLAUSE_FILLER = "編譯器需將每個來源條款轉換成獨立原子需求同時保存來源定位極性並提供可重現摘要識別"
262: _FILLER_ATOM = {"req_id": "REQ-FILLER", "owner": "PIPD-EC", "axis": "intent"}
263: 
264: # The unique obligation identities the three fixtures exercise.
265: SCALE_UNIQUE_OBLIGATIONS = (2, 237, 801)
266: 
267: 
268: def long_clause_scale_fixture(repeats: int = 60) -> dict:
269:     """One syntactically valid multi-thousand-character clause -> exactly one atom."""
270:     from pipd_ls_sp import requirements as req

273:     pi = {"subject_id": "PI-SCALE-LONG", "version": "1", "content_hash": "x" * 64,

68: ### The hard gate that is FAILING — stated here at the top, not buried
69: 
70: `python -B tools/perf_budget.py --check` → **FAIL**. `context_bytes_per_artefact` measures **343,547** against a
71: **20,000** budget (other runs: 336,674 / 355,119 — the exceedance is stable, it is not noise). All four thresholds carry
72: `source_of_truth: UNPROVENANCED`, so the three timing rows are `UNDECIDABLE`. Fail-closed behaviour was deliberately
73: retained and **no threshold was raised** — widening 20,000 to make the gate green would be weakening a gate.
74: The budget's derivation row is still missing (`TT-PIPD-PERF-CONTEXT-BUDGET-PROVENANCE`).

FILE /w/ACCEPTANCE.md
1: # External acceptance entry point — R5 S4 repair round (2026-10-10)
2: 
3: This branch was published so that an **independent verifier** can accept it. Everything here is written
4: for that reader. **Nothing in this repository is a claim of acceptance** — see the claim ceiling below.
5: 
6: | item | value |
7: |---|---|
8: | branch to accept | `r5-s4-packreader` |
9: | subject commit | the tip of branch `r5-s4-packreader`. Two bindings pin it, and neither depends on where a later commit sits: (a) the **sha256 of both subject files** (table below) — stable across any commit, and the same digests the independent checker verified; (b) `.hgk/ao/pub/PUBLICATION_SUBJECT_ATTESTATION_R5S4_PACKREADER.json`, which carries top-level `commit` and `tree` naming the exact subject it was computed over, seals the product and manifest digests, and adds a `payload_digest` so tampering is detectable. Evidence-only commits were added after that binding to deliver the acceptance-officer re-runs; they change no product file, and the digests in (a) are unaffected. The repository's older `.hgk/ao/pub/PUBLICATION_SUBJECT_ATTESTATION.json` attests the earlier R3 published subject and is deliberately left untouched. |
10: | previous public state (parent) | `7f13b90dc06354a0c6b7bd013f83a8cc244bf93d`; its tree is `git rev-parse 7f13b90^{tree}` |
11: | round | `R5-20261009-s4-user-operability` |
12: | scope | exactly one confirmed defect (**D1**) repaired, then independently checked |
13: | independent verdict | `.hgk/rounds/R5-20261009-s4-user-operability/d1repair/D1_INDEPENDENT_VERDICT_V2.json` |
14: | the contract that checker executed | `.hgk/rounds/R5-20261009-s4-user-operability/d1repair/CONTRACT-verify-D1.v2.md` |
15: | superseded first contract (kept deliberately) | `.hgk/rounds/R5-20261009-s4-user-operability/d1repair/CONTRACT-verify-D1.v1.md` |
16: | writer order | `.hgk/rounds/R5-20261009-s4-user-operability/d1repair/WO-WS-A-codex-writer.md` |
17: | provenance audit of the defect class | `.hgk/rounds/R5-20261009-s4-user-operability/far/D1_PROVENANCE_AUDIT.md` |
18: | evidence manifest — every round artifact, delivered or withheld, each with a hash | `.hgk/rounds/R5-20261009-s4-user-operability/EVIDENCE_MANIFEST.json` |
19: 
20: ## The defect (D1)
21: 
22: `tools/perf_budget.py` prints a `historical` block, commented as the recorded verdicts of earlier stages.
23: That block read **no stored value at all**: it re-measured the live corpus and printed the result as if it
24: were the record. The same field therefore took different values in different checkouts — observed live
25: readings `343547 / 368432 / 370091 / 372224 / 372935 / 375068` against a committed record of `343547` —
26: while the adjacent comment claimed the number was *preserved verbatim*. That contradicts the owner ruling
27: `S2_HISTORICAL_FAIL: PRESERVE` and the committed artifact `.hgk/artifacts/s2/PERF_BUDGET.json`
28: (`context_bytes_per_artefact = 343547`, verdict `FAIL`). `tests/test_perf_budget.py` could not catch it:
29: it hard-coded the expected number and asserted only the metric name.
30: 
31: ## What changed
32: 
33: 1. A `historical` row's `value` is now the **recorded** number, read at run time from the file its
34:    `origin {file, field}` names, with `recorded: true`. The live reading is **kept separately** in
35:    `measured_now`, so the drift is visible instead of disguised.
36: 2. A row with no recorded counterpart sets `recorded: false` and states in plain text that its value is a
37:    live measurement, not a recorded one. No record is fabricated.
38: 3. **No threshold, budget or voting metric changed** — verified byte-identical against the parent.
39: 4. Four regression tests were added; the expectation is read from the recorded file at test time and is
40:    never hard-coded.
41: 
42: ## Subject files, pinned by digest
43: 
44: | file | sha256 |
45: |---|---|
46: | `tools/perf_budget.py` | `f90c3d8d6061f55b9807c2e8740998f98124b56fbcb5c42397f459c4368ff015` |
47: | `tests/test_perf_budget.py` | `6c0d21fa8ce6c0a047539d2333973b88a75eea68c82ce031a96381749791d270` |

61: class TestRollback(unittest.TestCase):
62:     def test_rollback_pointer_is_a_real_git_commit(self) -> None:
63:         import subprocess
64:         head = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", str(ROOT), "rev-parse", "HEAD"],
65:                               capture_output=True, text=True)
66:         if head.returncode != 0:
67:             # Lane A found this test failing in a fresh copy that excludes .git. A missing git
68:             # checkout is an environment condition, not a product defect: skip, never pass silently.
69:             self.skipTest("no usable git checkout here (fresh copy excludes .git): "
70:                           f"git exited {head.returncode}")
71:         baseline = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", str(ROOT), "log", "--format=%H", "--reverse"],
72:                                   capture_output=True, text=True).stdout.split()
73:         self.assertTrue(baseline, "a baseline commit must exist for rollback")
74:         dirty_check = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", str(ROOT), "status", "--porcelain"],
75:                                      capture_output=True, text=True)
76:         self.assertEqual(dirty_check.returncode, 0)

78:     def test_repair_subject_must_also_be_inside_the_authorised_root(self) -> None:
79:         """A real defect found by the S2 golden pilot GP-03 negative set.
80: 
81:         `repair_candidate` validated the SCOPE but never the SUBJECT, so a caller could declare a
82:         benign scope such as `src/**` and point the subject at any path on the host. A bounded repair
83:         is only bounded if BOTH resolve inside the authorised root.
84:         """
85:         from pipd_ls_sp.errors import RepairScopeFail
86:         for bad_subject in ("C:/Windows/system32/x.dll", "/etc/passwd", "../../escape.py"):
87:             with self.subTest(subject=bad_subject):
88:                 with self.assertRaises(RepairScopeFail):
89:                     workspace.repair_candidate(bad_subject, scope=["src/**"], maker="M",
90:                                                authorized_root=str(ROOT))
91:         # a subject genuinely inside the root still succeeds
92:         ok = workspace.repair_candidate("src/pipd_ls_sp/util.py", scope=["src/**"], maker="M",
93:                                         authorized_root=str(ROOT))
94:         self.assertEqual(ok["state"], "CANDIDATE")
95:

98:         with self.assertRaises(DiffIncompatible):

256:         if getattr(args, "dry_run", False):
257:             return {"verdict": "PASS", "dry_run": True, "candidate": cand, "wrote_nothing": True,
258:                     "explain": {"derivation": ["dry-run: the scope was validated but nothing was written"]}}
259:         return cand
260:     raise AssertionError(cmd)
261: 
262: 
263: def _tracked_file_count(root: Path) -> int:
264:     """Count VCS-tracked files, or files in the tree when it is not a checkout.
265: 
266:     This used to be `sum(1 for _ in root.rglob("*") if _.is_file())`, which counted every object
267:     inside `.git/` - hundreds of internals that change on every repack - under a field literally
268:     named `tracked_files`. An independent checker caught the drift. Never count version-control
269:     internals as project files. The probe now lives in repo_context (R-AUD-009 derives the whole
270:     RepoContext there); this wrapper keeps the authoritative definition's exact behavior.
271:     """
272:     from . import repo_context
273:     return repo_context.tracked_file_count(root)
274: 
275: 
276: def _read_json(p: Path, what: str) -> dict:
277:     """Read a JSON input with TYPED failures.
278: 
279:     Before this, a missing or malformed input path escaped as FileNotFoundError /
280:     JSONDecodeError. `main()` only caught PipdError, so the CLI printed a traceback and exited 1 -
281:     an UNTYPED crash that a caller cannot branch on. A typed refusal is the whole contract of this
282:     surface, so I/O and parse failures are typed here.
283:     """
284:     from .errors import InputShapeInvalid, IoNotFound, ParseInvalid
285:     try:
286:         text = Path(p).read_text(encoding="utf-8")
287:     except OSError as exc:
288:         raise IoNotFound(f"cannot read {what} at {p}: {exc.strerror or type(exc).__name__}") from exc
289:     try:
290:         data = json.loads(text)
291:     except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
292:         raise ParseInvalid(f"{what} at {p} is not valid JSON: {exc.msg} at line {exc.lineno}") from exc
293:     if not isinstance(data, dict):
294:         raise InputShapeInvalid(f"{what} at {p} must be a JSON object, got {type(data).__name__}")
295:     return data
296: 
297: 
298: def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
299:     parser = build_parser()
300:     try:
301:         args = parser.parse_args(argv)
302:     except SystemExit as exc:
303:         # argparse exits 2 with a usage line on stderr and NOTHING on stdout. Publish the same
304:         # machine-readable envelope the domain refusals use, so every non-zero exit is branchable.
305:         if exc.code not in (0, None):

267:         absolute = raw.startswith("/") or bool(re.match(r"^[A-Za-z]:", raw))
